It is the first encyclical to be published since the Vatican decided to assert <a href="/wiki/Copyright" title="Copyright">copyright</a> in the official writings of the pope.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict's second encyclical titled <i>Spe Salvi</i> ("Saved by Hope"), about the virtue of <a href="/wiki/Hope" title="Hope">hope</a>, was released on 30 November 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> His third encyclical titled <i>Caritas in veritate</i> ("Love in Truth" or "Charity in Truth"), was signed on 29 June 2009 (the Feast of Sts. Peter and Paul) and released on 7 July 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-DonadoNYT_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DonadoNYT-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In it, the Pope continued the Church's teachings on social justice. He condemned the prevalent economic system "where the pernicious effects of sin are evident," and called on people to rediscover ethics in business and economic relations.<sup id="cite_ref-DonadoNYT_125-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DonadoNYT-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the time of his resignation, Benedict had completed a draft of a fourth encyclical entitled <i><a href="/wiki/Lumen_fidei" title="Lumen fidei">Lumen fidei</a></i> ("The Light of Faith"),<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> intended to accompany his first two encyclicals to complete a trilogy on the three <a href="/wiki/Theological_virtues" title="Theological virtues">theological virtues</a> of <a href="/wiki/Faith_in_Christianity" title="Faith in Christianity">faith</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hope_(virtue)" title="Hope (virtue)">hope</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Charity_(virtue)" class="mw-redirect" title="Charity (virtue)">love</a>. Benedict's successor, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Francis</a>, completed and published <i>Lumen Fidei</i> in June 2013, four months after Benedict's retirement and Francis's succession. Although the encyclical is officially the work of Francis, paragraph 7 of the encyclical explicitly expresses Francis's debt to Benedict: "These considerations on faith — in continuity with all that the Church's <a href="/wiki/Magisterium" title="Magisterium">magisterium</a> has pronounced on this theological virtue — are meant to supplement what Benedict XVI had written in his encyclical letters on charity and hope. He himself had almost completed a first draft of an encyclical on faith. For this I am deeply grateful to him, and as his brother in Christ I have taken up his fine work and added a few contributions of my own."<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-synodal_apostolic_exhortation">Post-synodal apostolic exhortation</span></h4>
<p><i><a href="/wiki/Sacramentum_caritatis" title="Sacramentum caritatis">Sacramentum caritatis</a></i> (The Sacrament of Charity), signed 22 February 2007, was released in Latin, Italian, English, French, German, Portuguese, Spanish, and Polish. It was made available in various languages on 13 March 2007 in Rome. The English edition of <i>Libera Editrice Vaticana</i> is 158 pages. This <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_exhortation" title="Apostolic exhortation">apostolic exhortation</a> "seeks to take up the richness and variety of the reflections and proposals which emerged from the Ordinary General Assembly of the <a href="/wiki/Synod_of_Bishops" class="mw-disambig" title="Synod of Bishops">Synod of Bishops</a>" which was held in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Motu_proprio_on_Tridentine_Mass"><i>Motu proprio</i> on Tridentine Mass</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Summorum_Pontificum" title="Summorum Pontificum">Summorum Pontificum</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Unchurch.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Unchurch.jpg/170px-Unchurch.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="259" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Unchurch.jpg/255px-Unchurch.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c9/Unchurch.jpg 2x" data-file-width="333" data-file-height="508" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Unchurch.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A pre-1969 <a href="/wiki/Traditional_Latin_Mass" class="mw-redirect" title="Traditional Latin Mass">Traditional Latin Mass</a> altar with <a href="/wiki/Reredos" title="Reredos">reredos</a></div></div></div>
<p>On 7 July 2007, Benedict issued the <i><a href="/wiki/Motu_proprio" title="Motu proprio">motu proprio</a> <a href="/wiki/Summorum_Pontificum" title="Summorum Pontificum">Summorum Pontificum</a></i>, declaring that upon "the request of the faithful", the celebration of <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a> according to the <a href="/wiki/1962_missal" class="mw-redirect" title="1962 missal">Missal of 1962</a> (of the <a href="/wiki/Tridentine_Mass" title="Tridentine Mass">Tridentine Mass</a>), was to be more easily permitted. Stable groups who previously had to petition their bishop to have a Tridentine Mass may now merely request permission from their local priest.<sup id="cite_ref-Afica_Latin_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Afica_Latin-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> While <i>Summorum Pontificum</i> directs that pastors should provide the Tridentine Mass upon the requests of the faithful, it also allows for any qualified priest to offer private celebrations of the Tridentine Mass, to which the faithful may be admitted if they wish.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_Motu_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter_Motu-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> For regularly scheduled public celebrations of the Tridentine Mass, the permission of the priest in charge of the church is required.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In an accompanying letter, the Pope outlined his position concerning questions about the new guidelines.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_Motu_130-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter_Motu-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> As there were fears that the move would entail a reversal of the Second Vatican Council,<sup id="cite_ref-Jason_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jason-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Benedict emphasised that the Tridentine Mass would not detract from the council and that the <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI" title="Mass of Paul VI">Mass of Paul VI</a> would still be the norm and priests were not permitted to refuse to say the Mass in that form. He pointed out that the use of Tridentine Mass "was never juridically abrogated and, consequently, in principle, was always permitted."<sup id="cite_ref-letter_Motu_130-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter_Motu-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The letter also decried "deformations of the <a href="/wiki/Liturgy" title="Liturgy">liturgy</a> ... because in many places celebrations were not faithful to the prescriptions of <a href="/wiki/Mass_of_Paul_VI" title="Mass of Paul VI">the new Missal</a>" as the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> was wrongly seen "as authorising or even requiring creativity", mentioning his own experience.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_Motu_130-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter_Motu-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Pope considered that allowing the Tridentine Mass to those who request it was a means to prevent or heal <a href="/wiki/Schism_(religion)" class="mw-redirect" title="Schism (religion)">schism</a>, stating that, on occasions in history, "not enough was done by the Church's leaders to maintain or regain reconciliation and unity" and that this "imposes an obligation on us today: to make every effort to enable for all those who truly desire unity to remain in that unity or to attain it anew."<sup id="cite_ref-letter_Motu_130-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter_Motu-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Dar%C3%ADo_Castrill%C3%B3n_Hoyos" title="Darío Castrillón Hoyos">Darío Castrillón Hoyos</a>, the president of the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Commission_Ecclesia_Dei" title="Pontifical Commission Ecclesia Dei">Pontifical Commission established to facilitate full ecclesial communion of those associated with that Society</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> stated that the decree "opened the door for their return". Bishop <a href="/wiki/Bernard_Fellay" title="Bernard Fellay">Bernard Fellay</a>, superior general of the SSPX, expressed "deep gratitude to the Sovereign Pontiff for this great spiritual benefit".<sup id="cite_ref-Afica_Latin_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Afica_Latin-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In July 2021, Pope Francis issued the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_letter" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic letter">apostolic letter</a> titled <i><a href="/wiki/Traditionis_custodes" title="Traditionis custodes">Traditionis custodes</a></i>, which substantially reversed the decision of Benedict XVI in <i>Summorum Pontificum</i> and imposed new and broad restrictions on the use of the <a href="/wiki/Traditional_Latin_Mass" class="mw-redirect" title="Traditional Latin Mass">Traditional Latin Mass</a>. The decision was controversial and widely criticized by conservative and <a href="/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholics" class="mw-redirect" title="Traditionalist Catholics">traditionalist Catholics</a> as lacking in charity and an attack on those attached to the liturgical patrimony of the Church.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Unicity_and_salvific_universality_of_the_Catholic_Church">Unicity and salvific universality of the Catholic Church</span></h4>
<p>Near the end of June 2007, the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith</a> issued a document approved by Benedict XVI "because some contemporary theological interpretations of <a href="/wiki/Vatican_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican II">Vatican II</a>'s ecumenical intent had been 'erroneous or ambiguous' and had prompted confusion and doubt." The document has been seen as restating "key sections of a 2000 text the pope wrote when he was prefect of the congregation, <i><a href="/wiki/Dominus_Iesus" title="Dominus Iesus">Dominus Iesus</a></i>."<sup id="cite_ref-Winfield_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Winfield-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Consumerism">Consumerism</span></h4>
<p>Benedict condemned excessive <a href="/wiki/Consumerism" title="Consumerism">consumerism</a>, especially among youth. He stated in December 2007 that "[A]dolescents, youths and even children are easy victims of the corruption of love, deceived by unscrupulous adults who, lying to themselves and to them, draw them into the dead-end streets of consumerism."<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> In June 2009, he blamed outsourcing for the greater availability of consumer goods which lead to the downsizing of <a href="/wiki/Social_security" class="mw-redirect" title="Social security">social security</a> systems.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecumenism">Ecumenism</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI_and_ecumenism" title="Pope Benedict XVI and ecumenism">Pope Benedict XVI and ecumenism</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg/220px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="316" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg/330px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg/440px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="713" data-file-height="1024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Benedict_XVI_1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict on a throne in the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Palace" title="Apostolic Palace">Apostolic Palace</a> in the Vatican</div></div></div>
<p>Speaking at his weekly audience in St. Peter's Square on 7 June 2006, Benedict asserted that Jesus himself had entrusted the leadership of the Church to his apostle <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Peter</a>. "Peter's responsibility thus consists of guaranteeing the communion with Christ. Let us pray so that the <a href="/wiki/Primacy_of_Peter" title="Primacy of Peter">primacy of Peter</a>, entrusted to poor human beings, may always be exercised in this original sense desired by the Lord, so that it will be increasingly recognised in its true meaning by brothers who are still not in <a href="/wiki/Koinonia" title="Koinonia">communion</a> with us."<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Also in 2006, Benedict met the <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a> <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury" title="Archbishop of Canterbury">Archbishop of Canterbury</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rowan_Williams" title="Rowan Williams">Rowan Williams</a>. In their Common Declaration, they highlighted the previous 40 years of dialogue between Catholics and Anglicans while also acknowledging "serious obstacles to our ecumenical progress".<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 4 November 2009, in response to a 2007 petition by the <a href="/wiki/Traditional_Anglican_Church" title="Traditional Anglican Church">Traditional Anglican Church</a>, Benedict issued the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_constitution" title="Apostolic constitution">apostolic constitution</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Anglicanorum_coetibus" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglicanorum coetibus">Anglicanorum coetibus</a></i>, which authorized the creation of "<a href="/wiki/Personal_ordinariate" title="Personal ordinariate">Personal Ordinariates for Anglicans entering into full communion</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Between 2011 and 2012, three ordinariates were erected, currently totaling 9090 members, 194 priests, and 94 parishes.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Interfaith_dialogue">Interfaith dialogue</span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Judaism">Judaism</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI_and_Judaism" title="Pope Benedict XVI and Judaism">Pope Benedict XVI and Judaism</a></div>
<p>When Benedict ascended to the papacy, his election was welcomed by the <a href="/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League" title="Anti-Defamation League">Anti-Defamation League</a> who noted "his great sensitivity to Jewish history and the <a href="/wiki/Holocaust" class="mw-redirect" title="Holocaust">Holocaust</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> However, his election received a more reserved response from <a href="/wiki/British_Chief_Rabbi" class="mw-redirect" title="British Chief Rabbi">British Chief Rabbi</a> <a href="/wiki/Jonathan_Sacks" class="mw-redirect" title="Jonathan Sacks">Jonathan Sacks</a>, who hoped that Benedict would "continue along the path of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a> and Pope John Paul II in working to enhance relations with the Jewish people and the <a href="/wiki/State_of_Israel" class="mw-redirect" title="State of Israel">State of Israel</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-BBC4462503_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC4462503-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Israeli foreign minister <a href="/wiki/Silvan_Shalom" title="Silvan Shalom">Silvan Shalom</a> also offered tentative praise, though Shalom believed that "this Pope, considering his historical experience, will be especially committed to an uncompromising fight against <a href="/wiki/Anti-Semitism" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-Semitism">anti-Semitism</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-BBC4462503_147-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC4462503-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Critics have accused Benedict's papacy of insensitivity towards Judaism. The two most prominent instances were the <a href="/wiki/Summorum_Pontificum" title="Summorum Pontificum">expansion of the use of the Tridentine Mass</a> and the lifting of the <a href="/wiki/Excommunication_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Excommunication (Catholic Church)">excommunication</a> on four bishops from the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_St._Pius_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of St. Pius X">Society of St. Pius X</a> (SSPX). In the <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday" title="Good Friday">Good Friday</a> service, the Tridentine Mass <a href="/wiki/Rubric" title="Rubric">rubrics</a> include a prayer that asks God to lift the veil so <i>they [Jews] may be delivered from their darkness</i>. This prayer has historically been contentious in <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_and_Judaism" title="Catholic Church and Judaism">Judaic-Catholic relations</a> and several groups saw the <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday_Prayer_for_the_Jews#Debate_after_the_Summorum_Pontificum_motu_proprio" class="mw-redirect" title="Good Friday Prayer for the Jews">restoration of the Tridentine Mass as problematic.</a><sup id="cite_ref-tomorrow_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tomorrow-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Among those whose excommunications were lifted was Bishop <a href="/wiki/Richard_Williamson_(bishop)" title="Richard Williamson (bishop)">Richard Williamson</a>, an outspoken <a href="/wiki/Historical_revisionism#Negationism_and_denial" title="Historical revisionism">historical revisionist</a> sometimes interpreted as a <a href="/wiki/Holocaust_denial" title="Holocaust denial">Holocaust denier</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> The lifting of his excommunication led critics to charge that the Pope was condoning his historical revisionist views.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Islam">Islam</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI_and_Islam" title="Pope Benedict XVI and Islam">Pope Benedict XVI and Islam</a></div>
<p>Benedict's relations with Islam were strained at times. On 12 September 2006, he delivered <a href="/wiki/Regensburg_lecture" title="Regensburg lecture">a lecture which touched on Islam at the University of Regensburg</a> in Germany. He had served there as a professor of theology before becoming Pope, and his lecture was entitled "Faith, Reason and the University—Memories and Reflections". The lecture received much attention from political and religious authorities. Many <a href="/wiki/Islamic_world" class="mw-redirect" title="Islamic world">Islamic politicians and religious leaders</a> registered their protest against what they labelled an insulting mischaracterisation of Islam, although his focus was aimed towards the rationality of religious violence, and its effect on the religion.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BBC1_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC1-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Muslims were particularly offended by a passage that the Pope quoted in his speech: "Show me just what Muhammad brought that was new and there you will find things only evil and inhuman, such as his command to spread by the sword the faith he preached."<sup id="cite_ref-BBC1_159-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC1-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The passage originally appeared in the <i>Dialogue Held with a Certain Persian, the Worthy Mouterizes, in Anakara of Galatia</i><sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> written in 1391 as an expression of the views of the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine" class="mw-redirect" title="Byzantine">Byzantine</a> emperor <a href="/wiki/Manuel_II_Paleologus" class="mw-redirect" title="Manuel II Paleologus">Manuel II Paleologus</a>, one of the last Christian rulers before the <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_Constantinople" title="Fall of Constantinople">Fall of Constantinople</a> to the Muslim <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a>, on such issues as <a href="/wiki/Forced_conversion" title="Forced conversion">forced conversion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Holy_war" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy war">holy war</a>, and the relationship between <a href="/wiki/Faith" title="Faith">faith</a> and <a href="/wiki/Reason" title="Reason">reason</a>. According to the German text, the Pope's original comment was that the emperor "addresses his interlocutor in an astoundingly harsh—to us surprisingly harsh—way" <i>(wendet er sich in erstaunlich schroffer, uns überraschend schroffer Form).</i><sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Benedict apologised for any offence he had caused and made a point of visiting <a href="/wiki/Turkey" title="Turkey">Turkey</a>, a predominantly Muslim country, and praying in its <a href="/wiki/Blue_Mosque,_Istanbul" title="Blue Mosque, Istanbul">Blue Mosque</a>. Benedict planned on 5 March 2008, to meet with Muslim scholars and religious leaders autumn 2008 at a Catholic-Muslim seminar in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> That meeting, the "First Meeting of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic-Muslim_Forum" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic-Muslim Forum">Catholic-Muslim Forum</a>," was held from 4–6 November 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> On 9 May 2009, Benedict visited the King Hussein Mosque in <a href="/wiki/Amman" title="Amman">Amman</a>, Jordan where he was addressed by <a href="/wiki/Prince_Ghazi_bin_Muhammad" title="Prince Ghazi bin Muhammad">Prince Ghazi bin Muhammad</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Signs_of_Hope_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Signs_of_Hope-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Buddhism">Buddhism</span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/14th_Dalai_Lama" title="14th Dalai Lama">Dalai Lama</a> congratulated Benedict XVI upon his election,<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> and visited him in October 2006 in Vatican City. In 2007, the People's Republic of China was accused of using its political influence to stop a meeting between the Pope and the Dalai Lama.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Indigenous_American_beliefs">Indigenous American beliefs</span></h4>
<p>While visiting Brazil in May 2007, "the pope sparked controversy by saying that native populations had been 'silently longing' for the Christian faith brought to South America by colonizers."<sup id="cite_ref-softenc_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-softenc-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> The Pope continued, stating that "the proclamation of Jesus and of his Gospel did not at any point involve an alienation of the <a href="/wiki/Pre-Columbian_era" title="Pre-Columbian era">pre-Columbus</a> cultures, nor was it the imposition of a foreign culture."<sup id="cite_ref-softenc_167-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-softenc-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Venezuelan president <a href="/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez" title="Hugo Chávez">Hugo Chávez</a> demanded an apology, and an indigenous organisation in Ecuador issued a response which stated that "representatives of the Catholic Church of those times, with honourable exceptions, were accomplices, deceivers and beneficiaries of one of the most horrific genocides of all humanity."<sup id="cite_ref-softenc_167-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-softenc-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Later, the Pope, speaking Italian, said at a weekly audience that it was "not possible to forget the suffering and the injustices inflicted by colonizers against the indigenous population, whose fundamental human rights were often trampled" but made no apology.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Hinduism">Hinduism</span></h4>
<p>While visiting the United States on 17 April 2008, Benedict met with <a href="/wiki/International_Society_for_Krishna_Consciousness" title="International Society for Krishna Consciousness">International Society for Krishna Consciousness</a> representative <a href="/wiki/Radhika_Ramana_Dasa" class="mw-redirect" title="Radhika Ramana Dasa">Radhika Ramana Dasa</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> a noted <a href="/wiki/Hindu" class="mw-redirect" title="Hindu">Hindu</a> scholar<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> and disciple of Hanumatpreshaka Swami.<sup id="cite_ref-Faculty_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faculty-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> On behalf of the Hindu American community, Radhika Ramana Dasa presented a gift of an <a href="/wiki/Om" title="Om">Om</a> symbol to Benedict.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Apostolic_ministry">Apostolic ministry</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_pastoral_visits_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="List of pastoral visits of Pope Benedict XVI">List of pastoral visits of Pope Benedict XVI</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg/220px-Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg/330px-Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg/440px-Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg 2x" data-file-width="783" data-file-height="518" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Popemobil_Mai_2007.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict in a <a href="/wiki/Mercedes-Benz" title="Mercedes-Benz">Mercedes-Benz</a> popemobile in <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo,_Brazil" class="mw-redirect" title="São Paulo, Brazil">São Paulo, Brazil</a></div></div></div>
<p>As pontiff, Benedict carried out numerous Apostolic activities including journeys across the world and in the Vatican.
</p><p>Benedict travelled extensively during the first three years of his papacy. In addition to his travels within Italy, he made two visits to his homeland, Germany, one for <a href="/wiki/World_Youth_Day" title="World Youth Day">World Youth Day</a> and another to visit the towns of his childhood. He also visited Poland and Spain, where he was enthusiastically received.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> His visit to Turkey, an overwhelmingly Muslim nation, was initially overshadowed by the <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI_Islam_controversy" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Benedict XVI Islam controversy">controversy about a lecture he had given at Regensburg</a>. His visit was met by nationalist and Islamic protesters<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> and was placed under unprecedented security measures.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> Benedict made a joint declaration with <a href="/wiki/Ecumenical_Patriarch_Bartholomew_I" class="mw-redirect" title="Ecumenical Patriarch Bartholomew I">Ecumenical Patriarch Bartholomew I</a> in an attempt to begin to heal the rift between the Catholic and <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox</a> churches.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2007, Benedict visited Brazil to address the Bishops' Conference there and canonize Friar Antônio Galvão, an 18th-century <a href="/wiki/Franciscan" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan">Franciscan</a>. In June 2007, Benedict made a personal <a href="/wiki/Christian_pilgrimage" title="Christian pilgrimage">pilgrimage</a> and pastoral visit to <a href="/wiki/Assisi" title="Assisi">Assisi</a>, the birthplace of <a href="/wiki/St._Francis_of_Assisi" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Francis of Assisi">St. Francis</a>. In September, Benedict undertook a three-day visit to Austria,<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> during which he joined Vienna's <a href="/wiki/Chief_rabbi" class="mw-redirect" title="Chief rabbi">chief rabbi</a>, Paul Chaim Eisenberg, in a memorial to the 65,000 <a href="/wiki/Viennese_Jews" class="mw-redirect" title="Viennese Jews">Viennese Jews</a> who perished in Nazi death camps.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> During his stay in Austria, he also celebrated Mass at the Marian shrine <a href="/wiki/Mariazell" title="Mariazell">Mariazell</a> and visited <a href="/wiki/Heiligenkreuz_Abbey" title="Heiligenkreuz Abbey">Heiligenkreuz Abbey</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:262px;"><a href="/wiki/File:President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg/260px-President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="173" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg/390px-President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg/520px-President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="667" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:President_George_W._Bush_and_Mrs._Laura_Bush_celebrate_the_81st_Birthday_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict celebrates his 81st birthday with U.S. president <a href="/wiki/George_W._Bush" title="George W. Bush">George W. Bush</a> and First Lady <a href="/wiki/Laura_Bush" title="Laura Bush">Laura Bush</a> at the <a href="/wiki/White_House" title="White House">White House</a>, Washington D.C.</div></div></div>
<p>In April 2008, Benedict made his <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI%27s_visit_to_the_United_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope Benedict XVI&#39;s visit to the United States">first visit to the United States</a> since becoming pope.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> He arrived in <a href="/wiki/Washington_D.C." class="mw-redirect" title="Washington D.C.">Washington D.C.</a> where he was formally received at the <a href="/wiki/White_House" title="White House">White House</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_meetings_between_the_pope_and_the_president_of_the_United_States" title="List of meetings between the pope and the president of the United States">met privately</a> with U.S. President <a href="/wiki/George_W._Bush" title="George W. Bush">George W. Bush</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> While in Washington, the pope addressed representatives of U.S. Catholic universities, met with leaders of other world religions, and celebrated Mass at the Washington Nationals' <a href="/wiki/Nationals_Park" title="Nationals Park">baseball stadium</a> with 47,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> The Pope also met privately with victims of sexual abuse by priests. The Pope travelled to <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York City</a> where he addressed the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Also while in New York, the Pope celebrated Mass at <a href="/wiki/St._Patrick%27s_Cathedral,_New_York" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Patrick&#39;s Cathedral, New York">St. Patrick's Cathedral</a>, met with disabled children and their families, and attended an event for Catholic youth, where he addressed some 25,000 young people in attendance.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> On the final day of the Pope's visit, he visited the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Center_site" title="World Trade Center site">World Trade Center site</a> and later celebrated Mass at <a href="/wiki/Yankee_Stadium_(1923)" title="Yankee Stadium (1923)">Yankee Stadium</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In July 2008, the Pope travelled to Australia to attend <a href="/wiki/World_Youth_Day_2008" title="World Youth Day 2008">World Youth Day 2008</a> in <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a>. On 19 July, in <a href="/wiki/St_Mary%27s_Cathedral,_Sydney" title="St Mary&#39;s Cathedral, Sydney">St. Mary's Cathedral</a>, he made an apology for child sex abuse perpetrated by the clergy in Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> On 13 September 2008, at an outdoor Paris Mass attended by 250,000 people, Benedict condemned the modern <a href="/wiki/Materialism" title="Materialism">materialism</a>&#160;– the world's love of power, possessions, and money as a modern-day plague, comparing it to <a href="/wiki/Paganism" title="Paganism">paganism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> In 2009, he visited Africa (<a href="/wiki/Cameroon" title="Cameroon">Cameroon</a> and <a href="/wiki/Angola" title="Angola">Angola</a>) for the first time as pope. During his visit, he suggested that altering sexual behavior was the answer to Africa's <a href="/wiki/AIDS" class="mw-redirect" title="AIDS">AIDS</a> crisis and urged Catholics to reach out and convert believers in <a href="/wiki/Magic_(paranormal)" class="mw-redirect" title="Magic (paranormal)">sorcery</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> He visited the Middle East (<a href="/wiki/Jordan" title="Jordan">Jordan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Israel" title="Israel">Israel</a>, and <a href="/wiki/State_of_Palestine" title="State of Palestine">Palestine</a>) in May 2009.
</p><p>Benedict's main arena for pastoral activity was the Vatican itself, his Christmas and Easter homilies and <a href="/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi" title="Urbi et Orbi">Urbi et Orbi</a> were delivered from St Peter's Basilica. The Vatican is also the only regular place where Benedict traveled via motor without the protective bulletproof case common to most <a href="/wiki/Popemobile" title="Popemobile">popemobiles</a>. Despite the more secure setting, Benedict was victim to security risks several times inside Vatican City. On Wednesday, 6 June 2007, during his General Audience, a man leapt across a barrier, evaded guards, and nearly mounted the Pope's vehicle, although he was stopped and Benedict seemed to be unaware of the event. On Thursday, 24 December 2009, while Benedict was proceeding to the altar to celebrate Christmas Eve Mass at <a href="/wiki/St_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="St Peter&#39;s Basilica">St Peter's Basilica</a>, a woman later identified as 25-year-old <a href="/wiki/Susanna_Maiolo" title="Susanna Maiolo">Susanna Maiolo</a>, who holds Italian and Swiss citizenship, jumped the barrier and grabbed the Pope by his <a href="/wiki/Papal_vestments" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal vestments">vestments</a> and pulled him to the ground. The 82-year-old Benedict fell but was assisted to his feet and he continued to proceed toward the altar to celebrate Mass. <a href="/wiki/Roger_Etchegaray" title="Roger Etchegaray">Roger Etchegaray</a>, the vice-dean of the College of Cardinals, fell as well and suffered a hip fracture. Italian police reported that Maiolo had in a prior action attempted to accost Benedict at the previous Christmas Eve Mass, but was prevented from doing so.<sup id="cite_ref-USAToday-PopeOK_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USAToday-PopeOK-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG/220px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="104" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG/330px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG/440px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3738" data-file-height="1769" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Malta.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict in <a href="/wiki/Balzan" title="Balzan">Balzan</a>, Malta</div></div></div>
<p>In his <a href="/wiki/Homily" title="Homily">homily</a>, Benedict forgave Susanna Maiolo<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> and urged the world to "wake up" from selfishness and petty affairs, and find time for God and spiritual matters.<sup id="cite_ref-USAToday-PopeOK_192-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USAToday-PopeOK-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg/220px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg/330px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg/440px-Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="449" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_Benedict_XVI_in_Zagreb_04.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict in <a href="/wiki/Zagreb" title="Zagreb">Zagreb</a>, Croatia</div></div></div>
<p>Between 17 and 18 April, 2010, Benedict made an Apostolic Journey to the Republic of Malta. Following meetings with various dignitaries on his first day on the island, 50,000 people gathered in a <a href="/wiki/Drizzle" title="Drizzle">drizzle</a> for Papal Mass on the granaries in <a href="/wiki/Floriana" title="Floriana">Floriana</a>. The Pope also met with the <a href="/wiki/Maltese_people" title="Maltese people">Maltese</a> youth at the <a href="/wiki/Valletta" title="Valletta">Valletta</a> Waterfront, where an estimated 10,000 young people turned up to greet him.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sexual_abuse_in_the_Catholic_Church">Sexual abuse in the Catholic Church</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Catholic_sex_abuse_cases" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholic sex abuse cases">Catholic sex abuse cases</a></div>
<p>Prior to 2001, the primary responsibility for investigating allegations of sexual abuse and disciplining perpetrators rested with the individual dioceses. In 2001, Ratzinger convinced John Paul II to put the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith in charge of all sexual abuse investigations.<sup id="cite_ref-times-7086738_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-times-7086738-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ncr-maciel_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncr-maciel-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/John_L._Allen_Jr." title="John L. Allen Jr.">John L. Allen Jr.</a>, Ratzinger in the following years "acquired a familiarity with the contours of the problem that virtually no other figure in the Catholic Church can claim. Driven by that encounter with what he would later refer to as 'filth' in the Church, Ratzinger seems to have undergone something of a 'conversion experience' throughout 2003–04. From that point forward, he and his staff seemed driven by a convert's zeal to clean up the mess."<sup id="cite_ref-ncr-1_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncr-1-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Vincent_Nichols" title="Vincent Nichols">Vincent Nichols</a> wrote that in his role as head of the CDF "[Ratzinger] led important changes made in church law: the inclusion in canon law of internet offences against children, the extension of child abuse offences to include the sexual abuse of all under 18, the case by case waiving of the statute of limitation and the establishment of a fast-track dismissal from the clerical state for offenders."<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Charles_J._Scicluna" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles J. Scicluna">Charles J. Scicluna</a>, a former prosecutor handling sexual abuse cases, "Cardinal Ratzinger displayed great wisdom and firmness in handling those cases, also demonstrating great courage in facing some of the most difficult and thorny cases, <i>sine acceptione personarum</i> [without respect of persons]".<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> According to Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Christoph_Sch%C3%B6nborn" title="Christoph Schönborn">Christoph Schönborn</a>, Ratzinger "made entirely clear efforts not to cover things up but to tackle and investigate them. This was not always met with approval in the Vatican".<sup id="cite_ref-times-7086738_196-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-times-7086738-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> Ratzinger had pressed John Paul II to investigate <a href="/wiki/Hans_Hermann_Gro%C3%ABr" title="Hans Hermann Groër">Hans Hermann Groër</a>, an Austrian cardinal and friend of John Paul accused of sexual abuse, which resulted in Groër's resignation.<sup id="cite_ref-independent-johnpaul_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-independent-johnpaul-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2010, the Pope sent a pastoral letter to the Catholic Church in Ireland addressing cases of sexual abuse by priests of minors, expressing sorrow, and promising changes in the way accusations of abuse were dealt with.<sup id="cite_ref-vatican.va_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vatican.va-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> Victims' groups claimed the letter failed to clarify if secular law enforcement had priority over canon law confidentiality regarding internal investigation of abuse allegations.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> The Pope then promised to introduce measures that would "safeguard young people in the future" and "bring to justice" priests who were responsible for abuse and the next month the Vatican issued guidelines on how existing church law should be implemented. The guidelines asserted that "Civil law concerning reporting of crimes&#160;... should always be followed."<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 2022, a report written by German law firm Westpfahl Spilker Wastl and commissioned by the Catholic Church concluded that Cardinal Ratzinger failed to adequately take action against clerics in four cases of alleged abuse while he was Archbishop of Munich and Freising from 1977 to 1982. The pope emeritus denied the accusations.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Benedict corrected his former statement that he had not been at a meeting of the ordinariate of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Munich_and_Freising" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Munich and Freising">Archdiocese of Munich and Freising</a> in January 1980, saying he mistakenly told German investigators he was not there. However, the error was "not done out of bad faith", but "the result of an error in the editorial processing" of his statement. According to <a href="/wiki/Reuters" title="Reuters">Reuters</a>, lawyer Martin Pusch said that "in a total of four cases, we have come to the conclusion that the then Archbishop Cardinal Ratzinger can be accused of misconduct in cases of sexual abuse."<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In February 2022, Benedict admitted that errors were made in the treating of sexual abuse cases when he was Archbishop of Munich. According to the letter released by the Vatican, he asked forgiveness for any "grievous fault" but denied personal wrongdoing. Benedict stated: "I have had great responsibilities in the Catholic Church. All the greater is my pain for the abuses and the errors that occurred in those different places during the time of my mandate."<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Legion_of_Christ_founder_Marcial_Maciel">Legion of Christ founder Marcial Maciel</span></h4>
<p>One of the cases Ratzinger pursued involved <a href="/wiki/Marcial_Maciel" title="Marcial Maciel">Marcial Maciel</a>, a Mexican priest and founder of the <a href="/wiki/Legionaries_of_Christ" title="Legionaries of Christ">Legionaries of Christ</a> who had been accused repeatedly of sexual abuse. Biographer Andrea Tornielli suggested that Cardinal Ratzinger had wanted to take action against Maciel but that John Paul II and other high-ranking officials, including several cardinals and the Pope's influential secretary <a href="/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Dziwisz" title="Stanisław Dziwisz">Stanisław Dziwisz</a>, prevented him from doing so.<sup id="cite_ref-ncr-maciel_197-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncr-maciel-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-independent-johnpaul_202-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-independent-johnpaul-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to <a href="/wiki/Jason_Berry" title="Jason Berry">Jason Berry</a>, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Sodano" title="Angelo Sodano">Angelo Sodano</a> "pressured" Ratzinger, who was "operating on the assumption that the charges were not justified", to halt the proceedings against Maciel in 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-nationalcatholicreporter.org_215-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nationalcatholicreporter.org-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> When Maciel was honored by the Pope in 2004, new accusers came forward<sup id="cite_ref-nationalcatholicreporter.org_215-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nationalcatholicreporter.org-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> and Cardinal Ratzinger "took it on himself to authorize an investigation of Maciel".<sup id="cite_ref-ncr-maciel_197-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncr-maciel-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> After Ratzinger became pope, he began proceedings against Maciel and the Legion of Christ that forced Maciel out of active service in the Church.<sup id="cite_ref-times-7086738_196-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-times-7086738-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> On 1 May 2010, the Vatican issued a statement denouncing "the most serious and objectively immoral behavior of Father Maciel, confirmed by incontrovertible witnesses, which amount to true crimes and show a life deprived of scruples and authentic religious feeling."<sup id="cite_ref-nyt1_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nyt1-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Theodore_McCarrick_controversy">Theodore McCarrick controversy</span></h4>
<p>In November 2020, the Vatican published a report blaming Popes John Paul II and Benedict XVI for allowing defrocked former cardinal <a href="/wiki/Theodore_McCarrick" title="Theodore McCarrick">Theodore McCarrick</a> to rise in power despite the fact that they both knew of sex abuse allegations against him.<sup id="cite_ref-benedictblame_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-benedictblame-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-tedpublic_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tedpublic-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Despite the fact that Benedict pressured McCarrick to resign as Archbishop of Washington D.C. in 2006, McCarrick remained very active in ministry throughout Benedict's papacy and even made a very public appearance when he presided over U.S. senator <a href="/wiki/Ted_Kennedy" title="Ted Kennedy">Ted Kennedy</a>'s burial service at Arlington National Cemetery in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-benedictblame_217-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-benedictblame-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-tedpublic_218-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tedpublic-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Attire">Attire</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ceremonial_of_Benedict_XVI" title="Ceremonial of Benedict XVI">Ceremonial of Benedict XVI</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedikt_XVI.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Benedikt_XVI.jpg/220px-Benedikt_XVI.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Benedikt_XVI.jpg/330px-Benedikt_XVI.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Benedikt_XVI.jpg/440px-Benedikt_XVI.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="960" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedikt_XVI.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict wearing a red <a href="/wiki/Cappello_romano" title="Cappello romano">Cappello romano</a> during an open-air Mass in front of <a href="/wiki/St_Peter%27s_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="St Peter&#39;s Basilica">St Peter's Basilica</a>, 2007</div></div></div>
<p>Benedict re-introduced several <a href="/wiki/Papal_garments" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal garments">papal garments</a> which had fallen into disuse. He resumed the use of the traditional red <a href="/wiki/Papal_shoes" title="Papal shoes">papal shoes</a>, which had been used since Roman times by popes but which had fallen into disuse during the pontificate of John Paul<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>II. Contrary to the initial speculation of the press that the shoes had been made by the Italian fashion house <a href="/wiki/Prada" title="Prada">Prada</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Vatican</a> announced that the shoes were provided by the Pope's personal shoemaker.<sup id="cite_ref-WSJ_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WSJ-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The journalist Charlotte Allen described Benedict as "the pope of aesthetics": "He has reminded a world that looks increasingly ugly and debased that there is such a thing as the beautiful—whether it's embodied in a sonata or an altarpiece or an embroidered cope or the cut of a cassock—and that earthly beauty ultimately communicates a beauty that is beyond earthly things."<sup id="cite_ref-latimes1_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-latimes1-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span></h3>
<p>Prior to his election as pope in 2005, Ratzinger had hoped to retire—on account of age-related health problems, a long-held desire to have free time to write, and the retirement age for bishops (75)—and submitted his resignation as Prefect of the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith three times, but continued at his post in obedience to the wishes of John Paul II. In September 1991, Ratzinger suffered a hemorrhagic stroke, which slightly impaired his eyesight temporarily but which he recovered completely.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> This was never officially made public—the official news was that he had fallen and struck his head against a radiator—but was an open secret known to the conclave that elected him pope.<sup id="cite_ref-op-online.de_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-op-online.de-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg/170px-BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="415" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg/255px-BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg/340px-BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg 2x" data-file-width="657" data-file-height="1602" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BentoXVI-29-10052007.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict in <a href="/wiki/Choir_dress" title="Choir dress">choir dress</a> with the red summer <a href="/wiki/Papal_mozzetta" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal mozzetta">papal mozzetta</a>, embroidered red <a href="/wiki/Stole_(vestment)" title="Stole (vestment)">stole</a>, and the red <a href="/wiki/Papal_shoes" title="Papal shoes">papal shoes</a></div></div></div>
<p>Following his election in April 2005 there were several rumors about the Pope's health, but none of them were confirmed. Early in his pontificate Benedict predicted a short reign, which led to concerns about his health.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> In May 2005 the Vatican announced that he had suffered another mild stroke. French cardinal <a href="/wiki/Philippe_Barbarin" title="Philippe Barbarin">Philippe Barbarin</a> said that since the first stroke Ratzinger had been suffering from an age-related heart condition, for which he was on medication. In late November 2006 Vatican insiders told the international press that the Pope had had a routine examination of the heart.<sup id="cite_ref-op-online.de_222-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-op-online.de-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> A few days later an unconfirmed rumor emerged that Benedict had undergone an operation in preparation for an eventual bypass operation, but this rumor was only published by a small left-wing Italian newspaper and was never confirmed by any Vatican insider.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 17 July 2009, Benedict was hospitalized after falling and breaking his right wrist while on vacation in the Alps; his injuries were reported to be minor.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the announcement of his resignation, the Vatican revealed that Benedict had been fitted with a <a href="/wiki/Pacemaker" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacemaker">pacemaker</a> while he was still a cardinal, before his election as pope in 2005. The battery in the pacemaker had been replaced three months earlier, a routine procedure, but that did not influence his decision.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Resignation">Resignation</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Resignation_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Resignation of Pope Benedict XVI">Resignation of Pope Benedict XVI</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg/220px-Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg/330px-Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg/440px-Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1026" data-file-height="722" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedict_XVI%27s_Last_Audience.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict in a <a href="/wiki/Popemobile" title="Popemobile">popemobile</a> at his final Wednesday General Audience in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a>, 27 February 2013</div></div></div>
<p>On 11 February 2013, the Vatican confirmed that Benedict would <a href="/wiki/Papal_resignation" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal resignation">resign the papacy</a> on 28 February 2013, as a result of his advanced age,<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> becoming the first pope to resign since <a href="/wiki/Gregory_XII" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregory XII">Gregory XII</a> in 1415.<sup id="cite_ref-resignCNN_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-resignCNN-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> At the age of 85 years and 318 days on the effective date of his retirement, he was the fourth-oldest person to hold the office of pope. The move was unexpected,<sup id="cite_ref-BBCresign1_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBCresign1-229">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> as all popes in modern times had held office until death. Benedict was the first pope to resign without external pressure since <a href="/wiki/Celestine_V" class="mw-redirect" title="Celestine V">Celestine V</a> in 1294.<sup id="cite_ref-FTstepDown_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FTstepDown-230">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his declaration of 10 February 2013, Benedict resigned as "Bishop of Rome, Successor of Saint Peter".<sup id="cite_ref-resignation-declaration_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-resignation-declaration-232">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> In a statement, he cited his deteriorating strength and the physical and mental demands of the papacy;<sup id="cite_ref-VatRadio_resign_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VatRadio_resign-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> addressing his cardinals in Latin, he gave a brief statement announcing his resignation. He also declared that he would continue to serve the Church "through a life dedicated to prayer".<sup id="cite_ref-VatRadio_resign_233-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VatRadio_resign-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to a statement from the Vatican, the timing of the resignation was not caused by any specific illness but was to "avoid that exhausting rush of Easter engagements".<sup id="cite_ref-Descrier_resign_234-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Descrier_resign-234">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> After two weeks of ceremonial farewells, the Pope left office at the appointed time and <i><a href="/wiki/Sede_vacante" title="Sede vacante">sede vacante</a></i> was declared. Benedict was succeeded by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jorge_Mario_Bergoglio" class="mw-redirect" title="Jorge Mario Bergoglio">Jorge Mario Bergoglio</a>, who took the papal name of Francis on 13 March 2013.
</p><p>On the eve of the first anniversary of Benedict's resignation he wrote to <i><a href="/wiki/La_Stampa" title="La Stampa">La Stampa</a></i> to deny speculation he had been forced to step down. "There isn't the slightest doubt about the validity of my resignation from the Petrine ministry," he wrote in a letter to the newspaper. "The only condition for the validity is the full freedom of the decision. Speculation about its invalidity is simply absurd," he wrote.<sup id="cite_ref-BenedictRejectsResignationTalk_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BenedictRejectsResignationTalk-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> In an interview on 28 February 2021, Benedict again repeated the legitimacy of his resignation.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Pope_emeritus:_2013.E2.80.932022"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Pope_emeritus:_2013–2022">Pope emeritus: 2013–2022</span></h2>
<p>On the morning of 28 February 2013, Benedict met with the full College of Cardinals and in the early afternoon flew by helicopter to the papal summer residence of <a href="/wiki/Papal_Palace_of_Castel_Gandolfo" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal Palace of Castel Gandolfo">Castel Gandolfo</a>. He stayed there until refurbishment was completed on his retirement home, the <a href="/wiki/Mater_Ecclesiae_Monastery" title="Mater Ecclesiae Monastery">Mater Ecclesiae Monastery</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Gardens" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Gardens">Vatican Gardens</a> near St Peter's, former home of twelve nuns, where he moved on 2 May 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-vanity_240-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vanity-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg/290px-5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="174" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg/435px-5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg/580px-5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:5_luglio_2013_-_incontro_tra_Papa_Francesco_e_Papa_Benedetto_XVI_-_inaugurazione_statua_San_Michele_Arcangelo_-.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope emeritus Benedict XVI with <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> (left) in the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Gardens" class="mw-redirect" title="Vatican Gardens">Vatican Gardens</a>, July 2013</div></div></div>
<p>After his resignation, Benedict retained his papal name rather than reverting to his birth name.<sup id="cite_ref-pope_emeritus_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pope_emeritus-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> He continued to wear the white cassock but without the <a href="/wiki/Pellegrina" title="Pellegrina">pellegrina</a> or the <a href="/wiki/Fascia_(sash)" title="Fascia (sash)">fascia</a>. He ceased wearing red <a href="/wiki/Papal_shoes" title="Papal shoes">papal shoes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> Benedict returned his official <a href="/wiki/Fisherman%27s_Ring" class="mw-redirect" title="Fisherman&#39;s Ring">Fisherman's Ring</a>, which was rendered unusable by making two large cuts across its face.<sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to a Vatican spokesman, Benedict spent his first day as <a href="/wiki/Pope_emeritus" class="mw-redirect" title="Pope emeritus">Pope emeritus</a> with Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Georg_G%C3%A4nswein" title="Georg Gänswein">Georg Gänswein</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prefect_of_the_Papal_Household" class="mw-redirect" title="Prefect of the Papal Household">Prefect of the Papal Household</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-244" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-244">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> In the monastery, the pope emeritus did not live a cloistered life, but studied and wrote.<sup id="cite_ref-vanity_240-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vanity-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> He joined Pope Francis several months later at the unveiling of a new statue of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Michael_the_Archangel" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Michael the Archangel">Saint Michael the Archangel</a>. The inscription on the statue, according to Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Lajolo" title="Giovanni Lajolo">Giovanni Lajolo</a>, has the coat of arms of the two popes to symbolize the fact that the statue was commissioned by Benedict and consecrated by Francis.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2013 it was reported that Benedict had multiple health problems including <a href="/wiki/High_blood_pressure" class="mw-redirect" title="High blood pressure">high blood pressure</a> and fallen out of bed more than once, but the Holy See denied any specific illnesses.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> The former pope made his first public appearance after his resignation at St. Peter's Basilica on 22 February 2014 to attend the first <a href="/wiki/Papal_consistory" title="Papal consistory">papal consistory</a> of his successor Francis. Benedict entered the basilica through a discreet entrance and was seated in a row with several other cardinals. He doffed his <a href="/wiki/Zucchetto" title="Zucchetto">zucchetto</a> when Francis came down the nave of St. Peter's Basilica to greet him.<sup id="cite_ref-247" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-247">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> He then made an appearance at the <a href="/wiki/Canonization_of_Pope_John_XXIII_and_Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Canonization of Pope John XXIII and Pope John Paul II">canonization mass of Popes John XXIII and John Paul II</a>, greeting the cardinals and Francis.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG/220px-Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="325" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG/330px-Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG/440px-Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1107" data-file-height="1634" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pope_emeritus_Benedict_XVI.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict in February 2014</div></div></div>
<p>In August 2014, Benedict celebrated Mass at the Vatican and met with his former doctoral students, an annual tradition he had kept since the 1970s.<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> He attended the <a href="/wiki/Beatification_of_Pope_Paul_VI" class="mw-redirect" title="Beatification of Pope Paul VI">beatification of Pope Paul VI</a> in October 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup> Weeks before this, he joined Francis in Saint Peter's Square for an audience with grandparents to honor their importance in society.<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict wrote the text of a speech, delivered by Archbishop Georg Gänswein, on the occasion of the dedication of the Aula Magna at the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_Urbaniana_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Pontifical Urbaniana University">Pontifical Urbaniana University</a> to the pope emeritus, "a gesture of gratitude for what he has done for the Church as a conciliar expert, with his teaching as professor, as Prefect of the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith and, finally, the Magisterium." The ceremony took place on Tuesday, 21 October 2014, during the opening of the academic year.<sup id="cite_ref-ZENIT_251-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ZENIT-251">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict attended the consistory for new cardinals in February 2015, greeting Francis at the beginning of the celebration.<sup id="cite_ref-252" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> In the summer of 2015, Benedict spent two weeks at Castel Gandolfo, at the invitation of Pope Francis. While at Castel Gandolfo, Benedict participated in two public events. He received two honorary doctorates given to him by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Stanislaw_Dziwisz" class="mw-redirect" title="Stanislaw Dziwisz">Stanislaw Dziwisz</a>, Pope John Paul II's longtime aide, from the <a href="/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_John_Paul_II" title="Pontifical University of John Paul II">Pontifical University of John Paul II</a> and the Kraków Academy of Music.<sup id="cite_ref-doctorates2015_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-doctorates2015-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> In his reception address, Benedict paid homage to his predecessor, John Paul II.<sup id="cite_ref-doctorates2015_253-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-doctorates2015-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Joseph Ratzinger–Benedict XVI Roman Library at the <a href="/wiki/Collegio_Teutonico" title="Collegio Teutonico">Pontifical Teutonic College</a> was announced in April 2015 and was scheduled to open to scholars in November 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-254" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-254">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup> The library section dedicated to his life and thought is being catalogued. It includes books by or about him and his studies, many donated by Benedict himself.<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-256" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict, in August 2015, submitted a handwritten card to act as a testimony to the cause of canonization of <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I" title="Pope John Paul I">Pope John Paul I</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-257" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-257">&#91;257&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-258" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-258">&#91;258&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2016, Benedict gave an interview expressing his views on <a href="/wiki/Mercy" title="Mercy">mercy</a> and endorsing Francis's stress on mercy in his pastoral practice.<sup id="cite_ref-259" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-259">&#91;259&#93;</a></sup> Also that month, a Vatican spokesman stated that Benedict was "slowly, serenely fading" in his physical health, although his mental capacity remained "perfectly lucid".<sup id="cite_ref-260" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-260">&#91;260&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The pope emeritus was honoured by the Roman Curia and Francis in 2016 at a special audience, honouring the <a href="/wiki/Sapphire_jubilee" title="Sapphire jubilee">65th anniversary</a> of his ordination to the priesthood. That November, he did not attend the consistory for new cardinals, rather meeting with them and Francis at his residence afterward.<sup id="cite_ref-261" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-261">&#91;261&#93;</a></sup> Following the death of Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Paulo_Evaristo_Arns" title="Paulo Evaristo Arns">Paulo Evaristo Arns</a> in December 2016, Benedict became the last living person appointed cardinal by Pope Paul VI.<sup id="cite_ref-262" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-262">&#91;262&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_%28cropped%29.jpg/220px-Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_%28cropped%29.jpg/440px-Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1011" data-file-height="749" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Meeting_with_Benedict_XVI_on_10_August_2019_(cropped).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict on 10 August 2019</div></div></div>
<p>In June 2017, Benedict received newly created cardinals in his chapel and spoke with each of them in their native language.<sup id="cite_ref-263" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-263">&#91;263&#93;</a></sup> In July 2017, he sent a message through his private secretary for the funeral of Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Joachim_Meisner" title="Joachim Meisner">Joachim Meisner</a>, who had suddenly died while on vacation in Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-264" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-264">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In November 2017, images emerged on the Facebook page of the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Passau" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Passau">Bishop of Passau</a>, Stefan Oster, of Benedict with a black eye; the bishop and author <a href="/wiki/Peter_Seewald" title="Peter Seewald">Peter Seewald</a> visited the former pope on 26 October since the pair were presenting Benedict with the new book <i>Benedict XVI – The German Pope</i> which the Passau diocese created. The former pope suffered the <a href="/wiki/Hematoma" title="Hematoma">hematoma</a> earlier after having slipped.<sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup>
