</p><p>In late 2019, Benedict collaborated on a book expressing that the Catholic Church must maintain its discipline of <a href="/wiki/Clerical_celibacy_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Clerical celibacy in the Catholic Church">clerical celibacy</a>, in light of ongoing debate on the issue, though later requested his name to be removed from the book as co-author.<sup id="cite_ref-266" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-266">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-267" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-267">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 2020, Benedict visited his dying brother Georg in Germany for the last time.<sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-270" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-270">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup> Georg died on 1 July, aged 96.<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 3 August 2020, Benedict's aides disclosed that he had an <a href="/wiki/Trigeminal_neuralgia" title="Trigeminal neuralgia">inflammation of the trigeminal nerve</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup> On 2 December of the same year, Maltese cardinal <a href="/wiki/Mario_Grech" title="Mario Grech">Mario Grech</a> announced to <i><a href="/wiki/Vatican_News" title="Vatican News">Vatican News</a></i> that Benedict had difficulty speaking and that he had told the new cardinals after the consistory that "the Lord has taken away my speech in order to let me appreciate silence".<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict became the longest-lived pope on 4 September 2020 at 93 years, 4 months, 16 days, surpassing the age of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup> In January 2021, Benedict and Francis each received doses of a <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_vaccine" title="COVID-19 vaccine">COVID-19 vaccine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> On 29 June 2021, the pope emeritus celebrated his <a href="/wiki/Platinum_Jubilee" class="mw-redirect" title="Platinum Jubilee">Platinum Jubilee</a> (70th anniversary) as a priest.<sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Francis#27_August_2022" title="Cardinals created by Francis">consistory of 27 August 2022</a>, Francis and the newly created cardinals paid a brief visit to Benedict at Mater Ecclesiae Monastery.<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death_and_funeral">Death and funeral</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Death_and_funeral_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Death and funeral of Pope Benedict XVI">Death and funeral of Pope Benedict XVI</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Worsening_health_and_death">Worsening health and death</span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1134653256">.mw-parser-output .side-box{margin:4px 0;box-sizing:border-box;border:1px solid #aaa;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em;background-color:#f9f9f9;display:flow-root}.mw-parser-output .side-box-abovebelow,.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{padding:0.25em 0.9em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-image{padding:2px 0 2px 0.9em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-imageright{padding:2px 0.9em 2px 0;text-align:center}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .side-box-flex{display:flex;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{flex:1}}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .side-box{width:238px}.mw-parser-output .side-box-right{clear:right;float:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-left{margin-right:1em}}</style><div class="side-box side-box-right plainlinks sistersitebox"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409"/>
<div class="side-box-flex">
<div class="side-box-image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Wikinews-logo.svg/40px-Wikinews-logo.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="22" class="noviewer" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Wikinews-logo.svg/60px-Wikinews-logo.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Wikinews-logo.svg/80px-Wikinews-logo.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="759" data-file-height="415" /></div>
<div class="side-box-text plainlist">Wikinews has related news:
<div><ul><li><a href="https://en.wikinews.org/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI_dies_at_age_95" class="extiw" title="wikinews:Pope Benedict XVI dies at age 95"> Pope Benedict XVI dies at age 95</a></li></ul></div></div></div>
</div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ratzinger_funeral_(01).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Ratzinger_funeral_%2801%29.jpg/220px-Ratzinger_funeral_%2801%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Ratzinger_funeral_%2801%29.jpg/330px-Ratzinger_funeral_%2801%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Ratzinger_funeral_%2801%29.jpg/440px-Ratzinger_funeral_%2801%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2378" data-file-height="1598" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ratzinger_funeral_(01).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Italian president <a href="/wiki/Sergio_Mattarella" title="Sergio Mattarella">Sergio Mattarella</a> paying respects to the body of Benedict XVI at <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a></div></div></div>
<p>On 28 December 2022, Pope Francis said at the end of his audience that Benedict was "very sick" and asked God to "comfort him and support him in this testimony of love for the Church until the end".<sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;279&#93;</a></sup> The same day, <a href="/wiki/Matteo_Bruni" title="Matteo Bruni">Matteo Bruni</a>, the director of the Holy See Press Office, stated that "in the last few hours there has been an aggravation of [Benedict's] health due to advancing age" and that he was under medical care. Bruni also stated that Francis visited Benedict at the Mater Ecclesiae Monastery after the audience.<sup id="cite_ref-280" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-280">&#91;280&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-281" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-281">&#91;281&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict died on 31 December 2022 at 9:34&#160;am <a href="/wiki/Central_European_Time" title="Central European Time">Central European Time</a> at the Mater Ecclesiae Monastery. He was 95 years old. His long-time secretary, Georg Gänswein, reported that his last words were "<span title="Italian-language text"><i lang="it">Signore ti amo</i></span>" (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a> for 'Lord, I love you&#39;).<sup id="cite_ref-282" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-282">&#91;282&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-283" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-283">&#91;283&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-284" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-284">&#91;284&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Funeral">Funeral</span></h3>
<p>From 2 to 4 January 2023, Benedict's body <a href="/wiki/Lay_in_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Lay in state">lay in state</a> in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">St. Peter's Basilica</a>, during which around 195,000 people paid their respects.<sup id="cite_ref-285" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-285">&#91;285&#93;</a></sup> His funeral took place on 5 January 2023 in <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a> at 9:30&#160;am, presided over by Pope Francis and celebrated by Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Re" title="Giovanni Battista Re">Giovanni Battista Re</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-286" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-286">&#91;286&#93;</a></sup> This was the first time since 1802 that a pope had attended a funeral for his predecessor.<sup id="cite_ref-287" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-287">&#91;287&#93;</a></sup> The funeral was attended by an estimated 50,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-Guardian_100k_288-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_100k-288">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup> Some attendees held signs reading or shouted "<i>Santo subito</i>", calling for his elevation to <a href="/wiki/Sainthood" class="mw-redirect" title="Sainthood">sainthood</a>, a cry heard previously at the funeral of John Paul II.<sup id="cite_ref-Newsbook_funeral_289-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newsbook_funeral-289">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup> Benedict was interred in the crypt beneath St. Peter's Basilica, in the same tomb originally occupied by John Paul II before his beatification in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-Guardian_100k_288-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_100k-288">&#91;288&#93;</a></sup> The tomb was opened to the public on 8 January 2023.<sup id="cite_ref-290" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-290">&#91;290&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Titles_and_styles">Titles and styles</span></h2><p>
As Pope, Benedict's rarely-used full title was:</p><blockquote><p><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a> Benedict XVI, <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Rome" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Rome">Bishop of Rome</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vicar_of_Jesus_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Vicar of Jesus Christ">Vicar of Jesus Christ</a>, <a href="/wiki/Successor_of_the_Prince_of_the_Apostles" class="mw-redirect" title="Successor of the Prince of the Apostles">Successor of the Prince of the Apostles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Pontiff_of_the_Universal_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Supreme Pontiff of the Universal Church">Supreme Pontiff of the Universal Church</a>, Primate of Italy, <a href="/wiki/Archbishop" title="Archbishop">Archbishop</a> and <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_of_the_Roman_Province" class="mw-redirect" title="Metropolitan of the Roman Province">Metropolitan of the Roman Province</a>, Sovereign of the Vatican City State, <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_the_servants_of_God" title="Servant of the servants of God">Servant of the servants of God</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-291" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-291">&#91;291&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote><p>The best-known title, that of "Pope", did not appear in the official list of titles, but is commonly used in the titles of documents, and appears, in abbreviated form, in their signatures as "PP." standing for "<i>Papa</i>" ("Pope").<sup id="cite_ref-292" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-292">&#91;292&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-293" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-293">&#91;293&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-294" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-294">&#91;294&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-295" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-295">&#91;295&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-296" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-296">&#91;296&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Before 1 March 2006, the list of titles also used to contain that of a "<a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_the_West" title="Patriarch of the West">Patriarch of the West</a>", which traditionally appeared in that list of titles before "Primate of Italy". The title of "Patriarch of the West" was removed in the 2006 edition of <i><a href="/wiki/Annuario_Pontificio" title="Annuario Pontificio">Annuario Pontificio</a></i>. According to <a href="/wiki/Achille_Silvestrini" title="Achille Silvestrini">Achille Silvestrini</a>, Benedict chose to remove the title at a time as a "sign of ecumenical sensitivity" on the issue of <a href="/wiki/Papal_primacy" title="Papal primacy">papal primacy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-patriarch_of_the_West_297-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-patriarch_of_the_West-297">&#91;297&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After his resignation, the official <a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">style</a> of the former pope in English was <i>His Holiness Benedict XVI, Supreme Pontiff emeritus</i> or <i>Pope emeritus</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-298" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-298">&#91;298&#93;</a></sup> Less formally he was referred to as Emeritus Pope or Roman Pontifex Emeritus.<sup id="cite_ref-299" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-299">&#91;299&#93;</a></sup> Moreover, according to the <a href="/wiki/1983_Code_of_Canon_Law" title="1983 Code of Canon Law">1983 <i>Code of Canon Law</i></a>, he was also <i>bishop emeritus of Rome</i>, retaining the sacred character received at his ordination as a bishop and receiving the title of emeritus of his diocese; although he did not use this style.<sup id="cite_ref-300" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-300">&#91;300&#93;</a></sup> The pope emeritus had personally preferred to be simply known as "Father".<sup id="cite_ref-301" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-301">&#91;301&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Positions_on_morality_and_politics">Positions on morality and politics</span></h2>
<h3><span id="Contraception_and_HIV.2FAIDS"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Contraception_and_HIV/AIDS">Contraception and HIV/AIDS</span></h3>
<p>In 2005, the Pope listed several ways to combat the spread of HIV, including chastity, fidelity in marriage, and anti-poverty efforts; he also rejected the use of condoms.<sup id="cite_ref-302" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-302">&#91;302&#93;</a></sup> The alleged Vatican investigation of whether there are any cases when married persons may use condoms to protect against the spread of infections surprised many Catholics in the wake of John Paul II's consistent refusal to consider condom use in response to AIDS.<sup id="cite_ref-303" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-303">&#91;303&#93;</a></sup> However, the Vatican has since stated that no such change in the Church's teaching can occur.<sup id="cite_ref-time.com_304-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time.com-304">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup> <i>TIME</i> also reported in its edition of 30 April 2006 that the Vatican's position remains what it always has been with Vatican officials "flatly dismiss[ing] reports that the Vatican is about to release a document that will condone any condom use."<sup id="cite_ref-time.com_304-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time.com-304">&#91;304&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2009, the Pope stated:
</p>
<blockquote><p>I would say that this problem of AIDS cannot be overcome merely with money, necessary though it is. If there is no human dimension, if Africans do not help, the problem cannot be overcome by the distribution of prophylactics: on the contrary, they increase it. The solution must have two elements: firstly, bringing out the human dimension of sexuality, that is to say a spiritual and human renewal that would bring with it a new way of behaving towards others, and secondly, true friendship offered above all to those who are suffering, a willingness to make sacrifices and to practise self-denial, to be alongside the suffering.<sup id="cite_ref-305" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-305">&#91;305&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>In November 2010, in a book-length interview, Benedict, using the example of male prostitutes, stated that the use of condoms, with the intention of reducing the risk of HIV infection, may be an indication that the prostitute is intending to reduce the evil connected with his immoral activity.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_306-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-306">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup> In the same interview, the Pope also reiterated the traditional teaching of the Church that condoms are not seen as a "real or moral solution" to the <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS_pandemic" class="mw-redirect" title="HIV/AIDS pandemic">HIV/AIDS pandemic</a>. Further, in December 2010, the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation of the Doctrine of the Faith">Congregation of the Doctrine of the Faith</a> explained that Benedict's statement did not constitute a legitimization of either contraception or prostitution, which remains gravely immoral.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_306-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-306">&#91;306&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Homosexuality">Homosexuality</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Homosexuality_and_the_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Homosexuality and the Catholic Church">Homosexuality and the Catholic Church</a></div>
<p>During his time as Prefect of the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith (CDF), Cardinal Ratzinger made several efforts to tackle the issue of homosexuality within the Catholic Church and the wider world. In 1986 the CDF sent a letter to all bishops entitled: <i><a href="/wiki/On_the_Pastoral_Care_of_Homosexual_Persons" title="On the Pastoral Care of Homosexual Persons">On the Pastoral Care of Homosexual Persons</a></i>. The letter condemned a liberal interpretation of the earlier CDF document <i>Declaration on Certain Questions Concerning Sexual Ethics</i>, which had led to a "benign" attitude "to the homosexual condition itself". <i>On the Pastoral Care of Homosexual Persons</i> clarified that the Church's position on homosexuality was that "although the particular inclination of the homosexual person is not a sin, it is a more or less strong tendency ordered toward an intrinsic moral evil; and thus the inclination itself must be seen as an objective disorder."<sup id="cite_ref-OPCHP_307-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OPCHP-307">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup> However, the document also condemned homophobic attacks and violence, stating that "It is deplorable that homosexual persons have been and are the object of violent malice in speech or in action. Such treatment deserves condemnation from the Church's pastors wherever it occurs."<sup id="cite_ref-OPCHP_307-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OPCHP-307">&#91;307&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1992, Ratzinger again approved CDF documents declaring that homosexual "inclination itself must be seen as an objective disorder" and extended this principle to civil law. "Sexual orientation", the document said, was not equivalent to race or ethnicity, and it declared that it was "not unjust discrimination to take sexual orientation into account."<sup id="cite_ref-gland_308-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gland-308">&#91;308&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 22 December 2008, Benedict gave an end-of-year message to the Roman Curia in which he talked about gender and the important distinction between men and women. He said that the Church viewed the distinction as central to human nature, and "asks that this order of creation be respected". In his words, the Church must "protect man from self-destruction". He said "something like a human ecology" was needed, and added: "Rain forests deserve indeed to be protected, but no less so does man." He attacked "gender theories", which he described as "man's attempt at self-emancipation from creation and the Creator."<sup id="cite_ref-309" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-309">&#91;309&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-gender_310-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gender-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-311" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-311">&#91;311&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/LGBT" title="LGBT">LGBT</a> groups, such as the Italian <a href="/wiki/Arcigay" title="Arcigay">Arcigay</a> and German <a href="/wiki/LSVD" title="LSVD">LSVD</a>, announced that they found Benedict's comments homophobic.<sup id="cite_ref-312" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-312">&#91;312&#93;</a></sup> Aurelio Mancuso, head of Arcigay, said "A divine programme for men and women is out of line with nature, where the roles are not so clear."<sup id="cite_ref-gender_310-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gender-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup> Canadian author <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Gawthrop_(writer)" title="Daniel Gawthrop (writer)">Daniel Gawthrop</a>, in a critical biography, <i>The Trial of Pope Benedict</i>, said that the Pope blamed homosexuality "for a problem the church had willingly enabled for hundreds of years".<sup id="cite_ref-313" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-313">&#91;313&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vatican spokesman <a href="/wiki/Federico_Lombardi" title="Federico Lombardi">Federico Lombardi</a> claimed the Pope had not wished specifically to attack people with homosexual inclinations, and had not mentioned gays or lesbians in his text. Lombardi insisted that there had been an overreaction to the pope's remarks, saying: "He was speaking more generally about gender theories which overlook the fundamental difference in creation between men and women and focus instead on cultural conditioning." Nevertheless, the remarks were interpreted as a call to save mankind from homosexuals and transsexuals.<sup id="cite_ref-gender_310-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gender-310">&#91;310&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Same-sex_marriage">Same-sex marriage</span></h4>
<p>In an address to a conference of the Diocese of Rome held at the basilica of <a href="/wiki/St._John_Lateran" class="mw-redirect" title="St. John Lateran">St. John Lateran</a> 6 June 2005, Benedict remarked on the issues of <a href="/wiki/Same-sex_marriage" title="Same-sex marriage">same-sex marriage</a> and abortion:<sup id="cite_ref-314" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-314">&#91;314&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The various forms of the dissolution of matrimony today, like free unions, trial marriages and going up to pseudo-matrimonies by people of the same sex, are rather expressions of an anarchic freedom that wrongly passes for true freedom of man...from here it becomes all the more clear how contrary it is to human love, to the profound vocation of man and woman, to systematically close their union to the gift of life, and even worse to suppress or tamper with the life that is born.</p></blockquote>
<p>During a 2012 Christmas speech,<sup id="cite_ref-315" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-315">&#91;315&#93;</a></sup> Benedict made remarks about the present-day interpretation of the notion of <a href="/wiki/Gender" title="Gender">gender</a>. He stated that a new philosophy of sexuality, which he rejects, suggests that "sex is no longer a given element of nature, that man has to accept and personally make sense of: it is a social role that we choose for ourselves", and "The words of the creation account: 'male and female he created them' (Gen 1:27) no longer apply". Although he did not mention the topic, his words were interpreted by news media as denunciations of <a href="/wiki/Same-sex_marriage" title="Same-sex marriage">same-sex marriage</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-316" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-316">&#91;316&#93;</a></sup> with some outlets adding that Benedict would have called it a threat to world peace similar to abortion and euthanasia.<sup id="cite_ref-317" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-317">&#91;317&#93;</a></sup> In March 2012, he stated that heterosexual marriages should be defended from "every possible misrepresentation of their true nature".<sup id="cite_ref-318" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-318">&#91;318&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="International_relations">International relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Holy_See" title="Foreign relations of the Holy See">Foreign relations of the Holy See</a></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Migrants_and_refugees">Migrants and refugees</span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg/290px-Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="194" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg/435px-Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fa/Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_in_the_Vatican_City_13_March_2007-4.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict with Russian president <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> in the Vatican, March 2007</div></div></div>
<p>In a message released 14 November 2006, during a Vatican press conference for the 2007 annual observance of <a href="/wiki/Refugee#World_Refugee_Day" title="Refugee">World Day for Migrants and Refugees</a>, the Pope urged the ratification of international conventions and policies that defend all migrants, including refugees, exiles, <a href="/wiki/Emergency_evacuation" title="Emergency evacuation">evacuees</a> and <a href="/wiki/Internally_displaced_person" title="Internally displaced person">internally displaced persons</a>. "The church encourages the ratification of the international legal instruments that aim to defend the rights of migrants, refugees and their families," the Pope said. "Much is already being done for the integration of the families of immigrants, although much still remains to be done."<sup id="cite_ref-319" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-319">&#91;319&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Benedict also promoted various UN events, such as <a href="/wiki/World_Refugee_Day" title="World Refugee Day">World Refugee Day</a>, on which he offered up special prayers for refugees and called for the international community to do more to secure refugees' human rights. He also called on Catholic communities and organizations to offer them concrete help.<sup id="cite_ref-320" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-320">&#91;320&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, it was reported that Benedict was "praying for migrants and refugees" from Syria.<sup id="cite_ref-321" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-321">&#91;321&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="China">China</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/China%E2%80%93Holy_See_relations" title="China–Holy See relations">China–Holy See relations</a></div>
<p>In 2007, Benedict sent a letter at Easter to Catholics in China that could have wide-ranging implications for the Church's relationship with China's leadership. The letter provides long-requested guidance to Chinese bishops on how to respond to illicitly ordained bishops, as well as how to strengthen ties with the <a href="/wiki/Chinese_Patriotic_Catholic_Association" class="mw-redirect" title="Chinese Patriotic Catholic Association">Patriotic Association</a> and the Communist government.<sup id="cite_ref-Letter_322-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Letter-322">&#91;322&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Korea">Korea</span></h4>
<p>On 13 November 2006, Benedict said that the dispute over the <a href="/wiki/North_Korea_nuclear_weapons_program" class="mw-redirect" title="North Korea nuclear weapons program">North Korea nuclear weapons program</a> should be resolved through negotiations, in his first public comment on the security issue, a news report said. "The Holy See encourages bilateral or multilateral negotiations, convinced that the solution must be sought through peaceful means and in respect for agreements taken by all sides to obtain the denuclearisation of the <a href="/wiki/Korean_Peninsula" class="mw-redirect" title="Korean Peninsula">Korean Peninsula</a>." Benedict was talking to the new Japanese ambassador to the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-323" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-323">&#91;323&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Turkey">Turkey</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Turkey_relations" title="Holy See–Turkey relations">Holy See–Turkey relations</a></div>
<p>In a 2004 <i><a href="/wiki/Le_Figaro" title="Le Figaro">Le Figaro</a></i> interview, Ratzinger said that Turkey, which is demographically Muslim but governmentally <a href="/wiki/Secularism_in_Turkey" title="Secularism in Turkey">secular by virtue of its state constitution</a>, should seek its future in an association of <a href="/wiki/Muslim_world" title="Muslim world">Muslim nations</a> rather than the European Union, which Ratzinger stated has Christian roots. He said Turkey had always been "in permanent contrast to Europe and that linking it to Europe would be a mistake".<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Later visiting the country to "reiterate the solidarity between the cultures," it was reported that Benedict made a counter-statement backing <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union" title="Accession of Turkey to the European Union">Turkey's bid to join the EU</a>. Turkish prime mimister <a href="/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan" title="Recep Tayyip Erdoğan">Recep Tayyip Erdoğan</a> said that the Pope told him in their meeting that while the Vatican seeks to stay out of politics, it desires Turkey's membership in the EU.<sup id="cite_ref-324" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-324">&#91;324&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-325" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-325">&#91;325&#93;</a></sup> However, the Common Declaration of Pope Benedict XVI and <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_Bartholomew_I_of_Constantinople" class="mw-redirect" title="Patriarch Bartholomew I of Constantinople">Patriarch Bartholomew I of Constantinople</a> implied that support for Turkey's membership in the European Union would be contingent on the establishment of religious freedom in Turkey:<sup id="cite_ref-326" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-326">&#91;326&#93;</a></sup> "In every step towards unification, minorities must be protected, with their cultural traditions and the distinguishing features of their religion."<sup id="cite_ref-:5_177-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Israel">Israel</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Israel_relations" title="Holy See–Israel relations">Holy See–Israel relations</a></div>
<p>In May 2009, Benedict visited Israel.<sup id="cite_ref-327" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-327">&#91;327&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-328" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-328">&#91;328&#93;</a></sup> This was the third Papal visit to the Holy Land, the previous ones being made by Paul VI in 1964 and John Paul II in 2000.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Vietnam">Vietnam</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Holy_See%E2%80%93Vietnam_relations" title="Holy See–Vietnam relations">Holy See–Vietnam relations</a></div>
<p>Vietnamese prime minister <a href="/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_T%E1%BA%A5n_D%C5%A9ng" title="Nguyễn Tấn Dũng">Nguyễn Tấn Dũng</a> met with Benedict at the Vatican on 25 January 2007 in a "new and important step towards establishing diplomatic ties".<sup id="cite_ref-329" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-329">&#91;329&#93;</a></sup> The Pope met with Vietnamese president <a href="/wiki/Nguy%E1%BB%85n_Minh_Tri%E1%BA%BFt" title="Nguyễn Minh Triết">Nguyễn Minh Triết</a> on 11 December 2009. Vatican officials called the meeting "a significant stage in the progress of bilateral relations with Vietnam."<sup id="cite_ref-330" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-330">&#91;330&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Global_economy">Global economy</span></h3>
<p>In July 2009, Benedict published his third encyclical, <i><a href="/wiki/Caritas_in_veritate" title="Caritas in veritate">Caritas in veritate</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-:0_331-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-331">&#91;331&#93;</a></sup> (<i>Charity in truth</i>), setting out the philosophical and moral foundations for human development, individually and collectively, in striving for the <a href="/wiki/Common_good" title="Common good">common good</a>. This was the last encyclical of Benedict XVI's papacy.
</p><p><i>Caritas in veritate</i> makes a case for the charitable distribution of wealth in considerable detail and discusses the environment, migration, terrorism, sexual tourism, bioethics, energy and population. <i>The Financial Times</i> reported that Benedict's advocacy for a fairer redistribution of wealth helped set the agenda for the 2009 July G8 summit.<sup id="cite_ref-332" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-332">&#91;332&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-333" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-333">&#91;333&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Also included in <i>Charity in Truth</i> is advocacy for <a href="/wiki/Tax_choice" title="Tax choice">tax choice</a>:
</p>
<blockquote><p>One possible approach to development aid would be to apply effectively what is known as fiscal subsidiarity, allowing citizens to decide how to allocate a portion of the taxes they pay to the State. Provided it does not degenerate into the promotion of special interests, this can help to stimulate forms of welfare solidarity from below, with obvious benefits in the area of solidarity for development as well.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_331-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-331">&#91;331&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nuclear_energy">Nuclear energy</span></h3>
<p>Benedict called for <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_disarmament" title="Nuclear disarmament">nuclear disarmament</a>. At the same time, he supported the peaceful use of <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_power" title="Nuclear power">nuclear energy</a> as a tool for development and the fight against poverty. In his message for the 50th anniversary of the founding of the <a href="/wiki/International_Atomic_Energy_Agency" title="International Atomic Energy Agency">International Atomic Energy Agency</a>, he confirmed: "The Holy See, fully approving of the IAEA's goal, has been a member from the organisation's foundation and continues to support its activity."<sup id="cite_ref-334" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-334">&#91;334&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg/170px-Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="327" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg/255px-Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/63/Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg 2x" data-file-width="340" data-file-height="653" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Beno%C3%AEt_XVI_synode_2008.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Benedict at the Synod of Bishops 2008 in Rome</div></div></div>
<p>Benedict was known to be deeply interested in classical music,<sup id="cite_ref-Willey_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Willey-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> and was an accomplished pianist.<sup id="cite_ref-335" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-335">&#91;335&#93;</a></sup> His favorite composer was <a href="/wiki/Wolfgang_Amadeus_Mozart" title="Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart">Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart</a>, of whose music he said: "His music is by no means just entertainment; it contains the whole tragedy of human existence."<sup id="cite_ref-MozartNCR_336-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MozartNCR-336">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup> Benedict also stated that Mozart's music affected him greatly as a young man and "deeply penetrated his soul".<sup id="cite_ref-MozartNCR_336-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MozartNCR-336">&#91;336&#93;</a></sup> Benedict's favorite works of music were Mozart's <a href="/wiki/Clarinet_Concerto_(Mozart)" title="Clarinet Concerto (Mozart)">Clarinet Concerto</a> and <a href="/wiki/Clarinet_Quintet_(Mozart)" title="Clarinet Quintet (Mozart)">Clarinet Quintet</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Mark_Freer_(2006)_337-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mark_Freer_(2006)-337">&#91;337&#93;</a></sup> He recorded <a href="/wiki/Alma_Mater_(Pope_Benedict_XVI_album)" title="Alma Mater (Pope Benedict XVI album)">an album</a> of contemporary classical music in which he sings and recites prayers to the <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Blessed Virgin Mary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-338" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-338">&#91;338&#93;</a></sup> The album was set for release on 30 November 2009. Benedict was also known to be fond of cats.<sup id="cite_ref-Willey_23-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Willey-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> As Cardinal Ratzinger, he was known (according to former neighbours) to look after <a href="/wiki/Feral_cat" title="Feral cat">stray cats</a> in his neighbourhood. A book called <i>Joseph and Chico: A Cat Recounts the Life of Pope Benedict XVI</i> was published in 2007 which told the story of the Pope's life from the feline Chico's perspective. This story was inspired by an orange tabby <a href="/wiki/Pentling" title="Pentling">Pentling</a> cat, which belonged to the family next door.<sup id="cite_ref-339" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-339">&#91;339&#93;</a></sup> During his trip to Australia for World Youth Day in 2008, the media reported that festival organizers lent the Pope a grey cat called Bella<sup id="cite_ref-340" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-340">&#91;340&#93;</a></sup> to keep him company during his stay.<sup id="cite_ref-341" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-341">&#91;341&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_networking">Social networking</span></h3>
<p>In December 2012, the Vatican announced that Benedict XVI had joined social networking website Twitter, under the handle @Pontifex.<sup id="cite_ref-342" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-342">&#91;342&#93;</a></sup> His first tweet was made on 12 December and was "Dear friends, I am pleased to get in touch with you through Twitter. Thank you for your generous response. I bless all of you from my heart."<sup id="cite_ref-343" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-343">&#91;343&#93;</a></sup> On 28 February 2013, the day he retired, the tweets were archived, and @Pontifex read "<a href="/wiki/Sede_Vacante" class="mw-redirect" title="Sede Vacante">Sede Vacante</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-344" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-344">&#91;344&#93;</a></sup> Pope Francis took control of the @Pontifex account upon his election.<sup id="cite_ref-345" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-345">&#91;345&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours_and_awards">Honours and awards</span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg/220px-20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg/330px-20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg/440px-20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:20130214_-_Notre-Dame_de_Paris_-_Les_nouvelles_cloches_-_007.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>In 2013, one of <a href="/wiki/Bells_of_Notre-Dame_de_Paris#21st_century_bells" title="Bells of Notre-Dame de Paris">Notre-Dame de Paris' new bells</a> was named Benoît-Joseph after Benedict.</div></div></div>
<p>A variety of awards and honours were given to Benedict including the following:
</p>
<ul><li>1999 Bailiff Grand Cross of Honour and Devotion of the <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_Military_Order_of_Malta" title="Sovereign Military Order of Malta">Sovereign Military Order of Malta</a><sup id="cite_ref-346" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-346">&#91;346&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>2010 honorary citizen of <a href="/wiki/Romano_Canavese" title="Romano Canavese">Romano Canavese</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Piedmont" title="Piedmont">Piedmont</a><sup id="cite_ref-347" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-347">&#91;347&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>2010 honorary citizen of <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a>, honoring his visit to the city on 11–12 May 2010<sup id="cite_ref-348" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-348">&#91;348&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The asteroid <a href="/wiki/8661_Ratzinger" title="8661 Ratzinger">8661 Ratzinger</a> was named in his honour for the role he played in supervising the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Apostolic_Archive" title="Vatican Apostolic Archive">Vatican Apostolic Archive</a> in 1998 to researchers investigating judicial errors against <a href="/wiki/Galileo_Galilei" title="Galileo Galilei">Galileo</a> and other scientists. The name was proposed by the asteroid's discoverers, L. D. Schmadel and F. Borngen at Tautenburg.<sup id="cite_ref-nasa_349-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nasa-349">&#91;349&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<dl><dt>Arms</dt></dl>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Coat of arms of Pope Benedict XVI">Coat of arms of Pope Benedict XVI</a></div>
<table class="wikitable mw-collapsible" style="max-width: 100%">
<caption class="nowrap">Coat of arms of Pope Benedict XVI
</caption>
<tbody><tr>
<td style="width: 220px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg" class="image"><img alt="Coat of Arms of Benedictus XVI.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg/220px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="295" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg/330px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg/440px-Coat_of_Arms_of_Benedictus_XVI.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="762" data-file-height="1021" /></a>
</td>
<td style="min-width:175px;">
<dl><dt>Notes</dt>
<dd>The <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Coat of arms of Pope Benedict XVI">coat of arms of Pope Benedict XVI</a> was designed by then Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Andrea_Cordero_Lanza_di_Montezemolo" title="Andrea Cordero Lanza di Montezemolo">Andrea Cordero Lanza di Montezemolo</a> (who later was created a Cardinal) soon after the papal election. Benedict's coat of arms omitted the <a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">papal tiara</a>, which traditionally appears in the background to designate the pope's position as a worldly ruler such as a king, replacing it with a simple <a href="/wiki/Mitre" title="Mitre">mitre</a>, emphasising his spiritual authority.<sup id="cite_ref-Vatican-COA_350-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vatican-COA-350">&#91;350&#93;</a></sup></dd>
<dt>Escutcheon</dt>
<dd>Gules, chape in or, with the scallop shell of the second; the dexter chape with a <a href="/wiki/Moor%27s_head" class="mw-redirect" title="Moor&#39;s head">Moor's head</a> in natural colour, crowned and collared of the first, the sinister chape a bear trippant in natural colour, carrying a pack gules belted sable</dd>
<dt>Symbolism</dt>
<dd><i>Scallop shell</i>: The symbolism of the scallop shell is multiple; one reference is to <a href="/wiki/Saint_Augustine" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Augustine">Saint Augustine</a>. While a doctoral candidate in 1953, Ratzinger wrote his thesis <i>The People and the House of God in St Augustine's Doctrine of the Church</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-351" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-351">&#91;351&#93;</a></sup> and therefore has a personal connection with the thought of this Doctor of the Church.<br /><i>Moor of Freising</i>: The Moor's head is an heraldic charge associated with Freising, Germany.<br /><i>Corbinian's bear</i>: A legend states that while travelling to Rome, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Corbinian" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Corbinian">Saint Corbinian</a>'s pack horse was killed by a bear. He commanded the bear to carry the load. Once he arrived, he released it from his service, and it returned to Bavaria. The implication is that "Christianity tamed and domesticated the ferocity of paganism and thus laid the foundations for a great civilisation in the Duchy of Bavaria." At the same time, Corbinian's bear, as God's beast of burden, symbolises the weight of office that Benedict carried.</dd></dl>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Writings">Writings</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI_bibliography" title="Pope Benedict XVI bibliography">Pope Benedict XVI bibliography</a></div>
<p>Benedict XVI wrote 66 books, three <a href="/wiki/Encyclicals" class="mw-redirect" title="Encyclicals">encyclicals</a>, and four <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_exhortation" title="Apostolic exhortation">apostolic exhortations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-352" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-352">&#91;352&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px 0;overflow:hidden;width:238px;border-collapse:collapse;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em}.mw-parser-output .ambox-speedy{border-left:10px solid #b32424;background-color:#fee7e6}.mw-parser-output .ambox-delete{border-left:10px solid #b32424}.mw-parser-output .ambox-content{border-left:10px solid #f28500}.mw-parser-output .ambox-style{border-left:10px solid #fc3}.mw-parser-output .ambox-move{border-left:10px solid #9932cc}.mw-parser-output .ambox-protection{border-left:10px solid #a2a9b1}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-text{border:none;padding:0.25em 0.5em;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-image{border:none;padding:2px 0 2px 0.5em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-imageright{border:none;padding:2px 0.5em 2px 0;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-empty-cell{border:none;padding:0;width:1px}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-image-div{width:52px}html.client-js body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Expand_section plainlinks metadata ambox mbox-small-left ambox-content" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg" class="image"><img alt="[icon]" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/20px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png" decoding="async" width="20" height="14" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/30px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg/40px-Wiki_letter_w_cropped.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="44" data-file-height="31" /></a></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs expansion</b>. You can help by <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Pope_Benedict_XVI&amp;action=edit&amp;section=">adding to it</a>.  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">January 2023</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>At his death, prior criticism of Benedict XVI received renewed attention, particularly that from public health officials, anti-AIDS activists, and victim's rights organizations over his handling of <a href="/wiki/Sexual_abuse_cases_within_the_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Sexual abuse cases within the Catholic Church">sexual abuse cases within the Catholic Church</a> and position on usage of <a href="/wiki/Condoms" class="mw-redirect" title="Condoms">condoms</a> in areas of <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Africa" title="HIV/AIDS in Africa">high HIV transmission</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_353-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-353">&#91;353&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:3_354-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-354">&#91;354&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-355" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-355">&#91;355&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Both Archbishop of Canterbury <a href="/wiki/Justin_Welby" title="Justin Welby">Justin Welby</a> and <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_Kirill_of_Moscow" title="Patriarch Kirill of Moscow">Patriarch Kirill of Moscow</a> expressed their condolences at Benedict's death. Welby referred to the former pope as "one of the greatest theologians of his age", while Kirill praised conciliatory efforts undertaken between the Catholic and Russian Orthodox churches during Benedict's pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-356" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-356">&#91;356&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some Catholics have called for Benedict's recognition as a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctor of the Church</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Newsbook_funeral_289-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newsbook_funeral-289">&#91;289&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-357" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-357">&#91;357&#93;</a></sup> with Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Gerhard_Ludwig_M%C3%BCller" title="Gerhard Ludwig Müller">Gerhard Müller</a> describing him as a "true doctor of the Church for today."<sup id="cite_ref-358" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-358">&#91;358&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
